\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(-[D|u|^{2}+B|v|^{2}]\Delta_{y}\Delta_{y}a+4a_{jk}Re(Du_{j}\bar{u}_{k}+Bv_{j}\bar{v}_{k})+L_{4}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}\Delta_{y}a\right)dy|v(t,x)|^{2}dx

\displaystyle-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}a\cdot\Im[\bar{u}(t,x)\nabla_{x}u(t,x)]dx[\nabla_{y}\cdot(\Im[A\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)+C\bar{v}(t,y)\nabla_{y}v(t,y)])]dy

\displaystyle-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{x}a\cdot\Im[\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)]dx[\nabla_{y}\cdot(\Im[B\bar{v}(t,y)\nabla_{y}v(t,y)+D\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)])]dy

\displaystyle-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{y}a\cdot\Im[\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)]dy[\nabla_{x}\cdot(\Im[A\bar{u}(t,x)\nabla_{x}u(t,x)+D\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)])])]dx

\displaystyle-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{y}a\cdot\Im[\bar{v}(t,y)\nabla_{y}v(t,y)]dy[\nabla_{x}\cdot(\Im[B\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)+C\bar{u}(t,x)\nabla_{x}u(t,x)])]dx.

(4.6)

If
d=3
and
a(x,y)=|x-y|
, denoting

\overline{\nabla}_{y}:=\nabla_{x}-\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}(\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}\cdot\nabla_{x}),\quad\overline{\nabla}_{x}:=\nabla_{y}-\frac{y-x}{|x-y|}(\frac{y-x}{|x-y|}\cdot\nabla_{y}),

then we have

\displaystyle\quad\frac{d}{dt}M^{\otimes_{2}}_{a}(t)

\displaystyle=16\pi\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[A|u(t,x)|^{4}+(C+D)|u|^{2}|v|^{2}+B|v(t,x)|^{4}]dx

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|u(t,x)|^{\alpha+2}|v(t,x)|^{\beta+2}[(L_{1}+L_{3})|u(t,y)|^{2}+(L_{2}+L_{4})|v(t,y)|^{2}]}{|x-y|}dxdy

\displaystyle\quad+4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{[A|\overline{\nabla}_{y}u(t,x)|^{2}+D|\overline{\nabla}_{y}v(t,x)|^{2}]|u(t,y)|^{2}}{|x-y|}dxdy

\displaystyle\quad+4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{[A|\overline{\nabla}_{x}u(t,y)|^{2}+C|\overline{\nabla}_{x}v(t,y)|^{2}]|u(t,x)|^{2}}{|x-y|}dxdy

\displaystyle\quad+4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{[B|\overline{\nabla}_{y}v(t,x)|^{2}+C|\overline{\nabla}_{y}u(t,x)|^{2}]|v(t,y)|^{2}}{|x-y|}dxdy

\displaystyle\quad+4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{[B|\overline{\nabla}_{x}v(t,y)|^{2}+D|\overline{\nabla}_{x}u(t,y)|^{2}]|v(t,x)|^{2}}{|x-y|}dxdy

\displaystyle\quad-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}\cdot\Im[\bar{u}(t,x)\nabla_{x}u(t,x)]dx[\nabla_{y}\cdot(\Im[A\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)+C\bar{v}(t,y)\nabla_{y}v(t,y)])]dy

\displaystyle\quad-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}\cdot\Im[\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)]dx[\nabla_{y}\cdot(\Im[B\bar{v}(t,y)\nabla_{y}v(t,y)+D\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)])]dy

\displaystyle\quad-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{y-x}{|x-y|}\cdot\Im[\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)]dy[\nabla_{x}\cdot(\Im[A\bar{u}(t,x)\nabla_{x}u(t,x)+D\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)])])]dx

\displaystyle\quad-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{y-x}{|x-y|}\cdot\Im[\bar{v}(t,y)\nabla_{y}v(t,y)]dy[\nabla_{x}\cdot(\Im[B\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)+C\bar{u}(t,x)\nabla_{x}u(t,x)])]dx

\displaystyle:=(I)+(II)+4(III)+4(IV)+4(V)+4(VI)-4(VII)-4(VIII)-4(X)-4(XI).

(4.7)

We will prove that

\displaystyle 4(III)+4(IV)+4(V)+4(VI)-4(VII)-4(VIII)-4(X)-4(XI)\geq 0.

(4.8)

We only estimate the term

-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}\cdot\Im[\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)]dx[\nabla_{y}\cdot(\Im[D\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)])]dy,

the other terms can be estimated similarly.

Denote
p_{1}(x)=2\Im[\bar{u}(t,x)\nabla_{x}u(t,x)]
,
p_{1}(y)=2\Im[\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)]
,
p_{2}(x)=2\Im[\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)]
and
p_{2}(y)=2\Im[\bar{v}(t,y)\nabla_{y}v(t,y)]
. Since

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\left(\frac{x_{j}-y_{j}}{|x-y|}\right)=\frac{\delta_{jk}}{|x-y|}-\frac{(x_{k}-y_{k})(x_{j}-y_{j})}{|x-y|^{3}},\quad j,k=1,2,3,

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{k}}\left(\frac{x_{j}-y_{j}}{|x-y|}\right)=-\frac{\delta_{jk}}{|x-y|}+\frac{(x_{k}-y_{k})(x_{j}-y_{j})}{|x-y|^{3}},\quad j,k=1,2,3,

integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\quad-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}\cdot\Im[\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)]dx[\nabla_{y}\cdot(\Im[D\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)])]dy

\displaystyle=D\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[p_{1}(y)p_{2}(x)-(p_{1}(y)\frac{x-y}{|x-y|})(p_{2}(x)\frac{x-y}{|x-y|})]\frac{dxdy}{|x-y|}.

(4.9)

Noticing that

\displaystyle|\pi_{(x-y)^{\perp}}p_{1}(y)|=|p_{1}(y)-\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}(\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}p_{1}(y))|\leq 2|\Im[\bar{u}(t,y)\overline{\nabla}_{x}u(t,y)]|\leq 2|u(t,y)||\overline{\nabla}_{x}u(t,y)|,

\displaystyle|\pi_{(y-x)^{\perp}}p_{2}(x)|=|p_{2}(x)-\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}(\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}p_{2}(x))|\leq 2|\Im[\bar{v}(t,x)\overline{\nabla}_{y}v(t,x)]|\leq 2|v(t,x)||\overline{\nabla}_{y}v(t,x)|,

(
4.9
) implies that

\displaystyle\quad-4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}\cdot\Im[\bar{v}(t,x)\nabla_{x}v(t,x)]dx[\nabla_{y}\cdot(\Im[D\bar{u}(t,y)\nabla_{y}u(t,y)])]dy

\displaystyle\geq-4D\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u(t,y)||\overline{\nabla}_{x}u(t,y)||v(t,x)||\overline{\nabla}_{y}v(t,x)|\frac{dxdy}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\geq-2D\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|u(t,y)|^{2}|\overline{\nabla}_{y}v(t,x)|^{2}}{|x-y|}dxdy-2D\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|v(t,x)|^{2}|\overline{\nabla}_{x}u(t,y)|^{2}}{|x-y|}dxdy.

Similarly, we also can obtain the results on -4(VI), -4(VII) and -4(VIII) as above. Summing them up, we get (
4.8
).

(
4.7
) and (
4.8
) mean that

\displaystyle\int_{I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u(t,x)|^{4}+|v(t,x)|^{4}]dxdt\lesssim|M^{\otimes_{2}}_{a}(t)|\lesssim\|u\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\int_{I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{|u(t,x)|^{\alpha+2}|v(t,x)|^{\beta+2}[|u(t,y)|^{2}+|v(t,y)|^{2}]}{|x-y|}dxdy

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(4.11)

Step 2. Estimate for
\|u(t,x)\|_{S^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v(t,x)\|_{S^{1}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}
.

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u(t,x)|^{4}+|v(t,x)|^{4}]dxdt<+\infty
, for any
0<\delta<1
, we can subdivide
(-\infty,+\infty)
into
J=J(E,M,\delta)
subintervals
J_{k}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{J_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u(t,x)|^{4}+|v(t,x)|^{4}]dxdt<\delta.

(4.12)

Note that

\displaystyle u(t)=\mathcal{J}(t)u_{0}-i\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}(t-s)[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u](s)ds,

(4.13)

\displaystyle v(t)=\mathcal{J}(t)v_{0}-i\mu\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}(t-s)[|u|^{\alpha+2}|u|^{\beta}v](s)ds.

(4.14)

Using Strichartz estimates, for any admissible pair
(q,r)
, we have

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{L^{q}((-\infty,\infty);W^{1,r}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\sum_{k=1}^{J}\|[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u]\|_{L^{2}(J_{k};W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})},

\displaystyle\|v(t)\|_{L^{q}((-\infty,\infty);W^{1,r}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\sum_{k=1}^{J}\|[|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v]\|_{L^{2}(J_{k};W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}.

Taking over all admissible pair
(q,r)
, we get

\displaystyle\qquad\|u(t)\|_{S^{1}((-\infty,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v(t)\|_{S^{1}((-\infty,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|v_{0}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{J}\left(\|[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u]\|_{L^{2}(J_{k};W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}+\|[|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v]\|_{L^{2}(J_{k};W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{J}\left\{\int_{J_{k}}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|u|^{\alpha+\beta+2}+|v|^{\alpha+\beta+2})^{\frac{6}{5}}(|u|+|\nabla u|+|v|+|\nabla v|)^{\frac{6}{5}}dx\right]^{\frac{5}{3}}dt\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|v_{0}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{J}\left\{\int_{J_{k}}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|u|^{2}+|v|^{2})dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{5}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|u|^{4}+|v|^{4})dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{6}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|u|^{6}+|v|^{6})dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{7}}}\right]^{\frac{5\tau_{4}}{3\tau_{2}}}dt\right\}^{\frac{1}{2\tau_{4}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left\{\int_{J_{k}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(|u|+|\nabla u|+|v|+|\nabla v|)^{\frac{6\tau_{1}}{5}}dx\right)^{\frac{5\tau_{3}}{3\tau_{1}}}dt\right\}^{\frac{1}{2\tau_{3}}}+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|v_{0}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{\frac{5-\tau_{1}}{4\tau_{1}}}\left(\|u(t)\|_{S^{1}((-\infty,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v(t)\|_{S^{1}((-\infty,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right)+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|v_{0}\|_{H^{1}},

(4.15)

which implies that

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{S^{1}((-\infty,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v(t)\|_{S^{1}((-\infty,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}<+\infty.

(4.16)

Here

\displaystyle\max\left(\frac{25}{13},\frac{5}{5-2(\alpha+\beta)},\frac{15}{7-2(\alpha+\beta)}\right)<\tau_{1}<5,\quad\tau_{2}=\frac{\tau_{1}}{\tau_{1}-1},\quad\tau_{3}=\frac{2\tau_{1}}{3\tau_{1}-5},\quad\tau_{4}=\frac{2\tau_{1}}{5-\tau_{1}},

\displaystyle\tau_{5}=\frac{20(\tau_{1}-1)}{[21-6(\alpha+\beta)]\tau_{1}-45},\quad\tau_{6}=\frac{10(\tau_{1}-1)}{3(5-\tau_{1})},\quad\tau_{7}=\frac{20(\tau_{1}-1)}{[5+6(\alpha+\beta)]\tau_{1}-5}.

Step 3.
H^{1}\times H^{1}
and
\Sigma\times\Sigma
scattering theories for (
1.6
).

For
0<t<+\infty
, we define

\displaystyle u_{+}(t)

\displaystyle=u_{0}-i\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}(-s)[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u](s)ds,

(4.17)

\displaystyle v_{+}(t)

\displaystyle=v_{0}-i\mu\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}(-s)[|u|^{\alpha+2}|u|^{\beta}v](s)ds.

(4.18)

Then for any
0<\tau<t
, we have

\displaystyle\|u_{+}(t)-u_{+}(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\int_{\tau}^{t}\mathcal{J}(t-s)[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u](s)ds\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}((\tau,t);H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))},

\displaystyle\|v_{+}(t)-v_{+}(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\int_{\tau}^{t}\mathcal{J}(t-s)[|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v](s)ds\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}((\tau,t);H^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}.

Using Strichartz estimates, and similar to (
4.15
), we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\|u_{+}(t)-u_{+}(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|v_{+}(t)-v_{+}(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u]\|_{L^{2}((\tau,t);W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}+\|[|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v]\|_{L^{2}((\tau,t);W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}

\displaystyle\leq\|u(t)\|_{S^{1}((\tau,t);\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v(t)\|_{S^{1}((\tau,t);\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Recalling (
4.16
), we know that for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
T=T(\epsilon)>0
such that

\|u_{+}(t)-u_{+}(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|v_{+}(t)-v_{+}(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\leq\epsilon\quad{\rm for\ any}\quad\tau,\ t>T(\epsilon),

which means that both
u_{+}(t)
and
v_{+}(t)
respectively converge in
H^{1}_{x}
as
t\rightarrow+\infty
to
u_{+}
and
v_{+}
as follows:

u_{+}:=u_{0}-i\lambda\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{J}(-s)[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u](s)ds,\quad v_{+}:=v_{0}-i\mu\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{J}(-s)[|u|^{\alpha+2}|u|^{\beta}v](s)ds.

Moreover, by Strichartz estimates, (
4.15
) and (
4.16
), we can get

\displaystyle\quad\|e^{-it\Delta}u(t)-u_{+}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|e^{-it\Delta}v(t)-v_{+}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\|\lambda\int_{t}^{+\infty}\mathcal{J}(-s)[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u](s)ds\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|\mu\int_{t}^{+\infty}\mathcal{J}(-s)[|u|^{\alpha+2}|u|^{\beta}v](s)ds\|_{H^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u(t)\|_{S^{1}((t,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v(t)\|_{S^{1}((t,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\ t\rightarrow+\infty.

Similarly, we can prove that there exist
u_{-}
and
v_{-}
such that

\displaystyle\|e^{-it\Delta}u(t)-u_{-}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|e^{-it\Delta}v(t)-v_{-}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\ t\rightarrow-\infty.

(
1.23
) and (
1.24
) are obtained.

Now we will prove (
1.25
) and (
1.26
). Letting
w(t)=(x+2it\nabla)u
,
z(t)=(x+2it\nabla)v
, we get the following Duhamel formulae

\displaystyle w(t)=\mathcal{J}(t)(xu_{0})-i\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}(t-s)\tilde{f}_{1}(u,v,w,z,)(s)ds,

(4.19)

\displaystyle z(t)=\mathcal{J}(t)(xv_{0})-i\mu\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{J}(t-s)\tilde{f}_{2}(u,v,w,,z,)(s)ds.

(4.20)

Here

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{1}(u,v,w,z)=(\frac{\alpha}{2}+1)|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}w-\frac{\alpha u}{2\bar{u}}|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}\bar{w}+(\frac{\beta}{2}+1)|u|^{\alpha}u|v|^{\beta}(\bar{v}z-v\bar{z}),

(4.21)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{2}(u,v,w,z)=(\frac{\beta}{2}+1)|v|^{\beta}|u|^{\alpha+2}z-\frac{\beta v}{2\bar{v}}|v|^{\beta}|u|^{\alpha+2}\bar{z}+(\frac{\alpha}{2}+1)|v|^{\beta}v|u|^{\alpha}(\bar{u}w-u\bar{w}).

(4.22)

Noticing that
|\bar{u}|=|u|
,
|\bar{v}|=|v|
,
|\bar{w}|=|w|
,
|\bar{z}|=|z|
and

|u|^{\alpha+1}|v|^{\beta+1}\lesssim|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}+|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta},

using Strichartz estimates and similar to (
4.15
), we can obtain

\displaystyle\|w(t)\|_{S^{0}((-\infty,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|z(t)\|_{S^{0}((-\infty,+\infty);\mathbb{R}^{3})}<+\infty,

(4.23)

and

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}_{1}(u,v,w,z,)\|_{L^{2}((-\infty,+\infty),L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}+\|\tilde{f}_{2}(u,v,w,z)\|_{L^{2}((-\infty,+\infty),L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}<+\infty.

(4.24)

Letting
\tilde{u}(t)=e^{-it\Delta}u(t)
and
\tilde{v}(t)=e^{-it\Delta}v(t)
, we get

\displaystyle x\tilde{u}(t)-x\tilde{u}(\tau)=i\lambda\int^{t}_{\tau}e^{-is\Delta}\tilde{f}_{1}(u,v,w,z)(s)ds,

(4.25)

\displaystyle x\tilde{v}(t)-x\tilde{v}(\tau)=i\lambda\int^{t}_{\tau}e^{-is\Delta}\tilde{f}_{2}(u,v,w,z)(s)ds.

(4.26)

Consequently, using (
4.23
)–(
4.26
) and Strichartz estimates, we have

\displaystyle\quad\|x\tilde{u}(t)-x\tilde{u}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+\|x\tilde{v}(t)-x\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{f}_{1}(u,v,w,z)\|_{L^{2}((t,\tau),L^{\frac{6}{5}})}+\|\tilde{f}_{1}(u,v,w,z)\|_{L^{2}((t,\tau),L^{\frac{6}{5}})}\longrightarrow 0

(4.27)

as
t,\tau\rightarrow+\infty
, which implies that

\displaystyle\|x\tilde{u}(t)-xu_{+}\|_{L^{2}}+\|x\tilde{v}(t)-xv_{+}\|_{L^{2}}\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad t\rightarrow+\infty.

(4.28)

Combining (
1.23
) and (
4.28
), we can get (
1.25
).

Similarly, (
1.26
) can be obtained. Theorem 4 is proved.
\Box

4.2
\Sigma\times\Sigma
scattering theory for (
1.6
) with defocusing nonlinearities and
\alpha+\beta=2
excluding the endpoints when
d=3

In this subsection, we will establish
\Sigma\times\Sigma
scattering theory for (
1.6
) with defocusing nonlinearities(i.e.,
\lambda>0
,
\mu>0
) and
\alpha+\beta=2
excluding the endpoints when
d=3
. That is, the exponents pair
(\alpha,\beta)
is on the critical exponents line
\alpha+\beta=2
excluding the endpoints
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
and
(\alpha,\beta)=(2,0)
. In this case, the method used in the subcritical case is invalid, the essential difficulty in technique is that we cannot obtain the expected estimates when we use Hölder’s inequality. We will use the weight-coupled pseudo-conformal conservation law to give some estimates below.

Lemma 4.1(Weight-coupled pseudo-conformal conservation law).

Assume that
(u,v)
is the global solution of (
1.6
) with defocusing nonlinearities when
d=3
,
\alpha>0
,
\beta>0
,
\alpha+\beta=2
and
(u_{0},v_{0})\in\Sigma\times\Sigma
. Then

\displaystyle P(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}|(x+2it\nabla)u|^{2}+c_{2}|(x+2it\nabla)v|^{2}]dx+4t^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}|xu_{0}|^{2}+c_{2}|xv_{0}|^{2}]dx-16\int_{0}^{t}\tau\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dxd\tau.

(4.29)

Here
(c_{1},c_{2})=(\frac{\alpha+2}{2\lambda},\frac{\beta+2}{2\mu})
.

Proof:
Using
E_{w}(u,v)=E_{w}(u_{0},v_{0})
, we get

\displaystyle P(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}|xu|^{2}+c_{2}|xv|^{2}]dx-4t\Im\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}\bar{u}(x\cdot\nabla u)+c_{2}\bar{v}(x\cdot\nabla v)]dx+4t^{2}E_{w}(u_{0},v_{0}).

(4.30)

Recalling that

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|x|^{2}|u|^{2}dx=4\Im\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bar{u}(x\cdot\nabla u)dx,\quad\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|x|^{2}|v|^{2}dx=4\Im\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bar{v}(x\cdot\nabla v)dx,

we obtain

\displaystyle P^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}|xu|^{2}+c_{2}|xv|^{2}]dx-4\Im\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}\bar{u}(x\cdot\nabla u)+c_{2}\bar{v}(x\cdot\nabla v)]dx

\displaystyle\quad-4t\frac{d}{dt}\Im\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}\bar{u}(x\cdot\nabla u)+c_{2}\bar{v}(x\cdot\nabla v)]dx+8tE_{w}(u_{0},v_{0})

\displaystyle=-4t\frac{d}{dt}\Im\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}\bar{u}(x\cdot\nabla u)+c_{2}\bar{v}(x\cdot\nabla v)]dx+8tE_{w}(u_{0},v_{0})

\displaystyle=-16t\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx.

(4.31)

Integrating (
4.31
) from
0
to
t
, we have (
4.29
).
\Box

As the direct result of (
4.29
), we give the decay rate of the global solution
(u,v)
of (
1.6
).

Lemma 4.2(Decay rate of the global solution).

Assume that
(u,v)
is the global solution of (
1.6
) with defocusing nonlinearities when
d=3
,
\alpha>0
,
\beta>0
,
\alpha+\beta=2
and
(u_{0},v_{0})\in\Sigma\times\Sigma
.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx\leq\frac{C}{t^{2}}.

(4.32)

Proof:
By (
4.29
), we have

\displaystyle t^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[c_{1}|xu_{0}|^{2}+c_{2}|xv_{0}|^{2}]dx\leq C,

(4.33)

which implies (
4.32
).
\Box

Now we will establish the
\Sigma\times\Sigma
scattering result for the solution of (
1.6
).

Note that
(2,6)
is an admissible pair
(q,r)
when
d=3
.

We first prove that if
\alpha>0
,
\beta>0
and
\alpha+\beta=2
, i.e.,
(\alpha,\beta)
is on the critical exponents line
\alpha+\beta=2
excluding the endpoints
(0,2)
and
(2,0)
, then

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}+\|v\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}\leq C\quad{\rm for}\quad t>0.

(4.34)

Using Duhamel’s principle and Strichartz estimates with
(q,r)=(2,6)
and
(q^{\prime},r^{\prime})=(2,\frac{6}{5})
, and noticing that

|u|^{\alpha+1}|v|^{\beta+1}\leq C[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}+|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}],

we have

\displaystyle\quad\|u\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}+\|v\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+C\|v_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+C\||u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}+C\||u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}

\displaystyle\leq C+C\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}+|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}]^{\frac{3}{2}}dx\right)^{\frac{4}{5}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u|+|v|+|\nabla u|+|\nabla v|]^{6}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C+C\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}+|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}]^{\frac{3}{2}}dx\right)^{\frac{4}{5}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u|+|v|+|\nabla u|+|\nabla v|]^{6}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\left(\int_{T}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}+|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}]^{\frac{3}{2}}dx\right)^{\frac{4}{5}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u|+|v|+|\nabla u|+|\nabla v|]^{6}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}+C\left(\int_{T}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{6}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{2}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u|+|v|+|\nabla u|+|\nabla v|]^{6}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\left(\int_{T}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{3}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{6}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{4}}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u|+|v|+|\nabla u|+|\nabla v|]^{6}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}+\max_{(T,t)}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tau_{3}}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times[\|u\|_{L^{2}((T,t),W^{1,6})}+\|v\|_{L^{2}((T,t),W^{1,6})}]

\displaystyle\leq C+\frac{1}{2}[\|u\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}+\|v\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}]

(4.35)

because
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta+2}dx
can be small enough if
\alpha>0
,
\beta>0
,
\alpha+\beta=2
when
t
and
T
are large while
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|u|^{6}dx
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v|^{6}dx
are bounded for all
t\geq 0
. Here

\frac{1}{\tau_{1}}=\frac{3\alpha}{2\alpha+4},\quad\frac{1}{\tau_{2}}=\frac{4-\alpha}{2\alpha+4},\quad\frac{1}{\tau_{3}}=\frac{3\beta}{2\beta+4},\quad\frac{1}{\tau_{4}}=\frac{4-\beta}{2\beta+4}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}+\|v\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}\leq C\quad{\rm for\ all}\ t\geq 0.

(4.36)

As a byproduct of (
4.35
), we get

\displaystyle\||u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}+\||u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}\leq C,\quad t>0,

which implies that

\displaystyle\||u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u\|_{L^{2}((t,\tau),W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}+\||u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v\|_{L^{2}((t,\tau),W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}\longrightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad t,\ \tau\rightarrow+\infty.

(4.37)

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\|e^{-it\Delta}u(t)-e^{-i\tau\Delta}u(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|e^{-it\Delta}v(t)-e^{-i\tau\Delta}v(\tau)\|_{H^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\int_{t}^{\tau}e^{-is\Delta}|u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u(s)ds\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|\int_{t}^{\tau}e^{-is\Delta}|u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v(s)ds\|_{H^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C\||u|^{\alpha}|v|^{\beta+2}u\|_{L^{2}((t,\tau),W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}+C\||u|^{\alpha+2}|v|^{\beta}v\|_{L^{2}((t,\tau),W^{1,\frac{6}{5}})}

\displaystyle\quad\longrightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad t,\ \tau\rightarrow+\infty,

(4.38)

which implies that there exists
(u_{+},v_{+})
such that

\displaystyle\|e^{-it\Delta}u(t)-u_{+}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}+\|e^{-it\Delta}v(t)-v_{+}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\longrightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad t,\ \tau\rightarrow+\infty.

(4.39)

Letting
w
,
z
,
\tilde{f}_{1}(u,v,w,z)
and
\tilde{f}_{2}(u,v,w,z)
be defined as in (
4.19
)–(
4.23
), the difference is that
\alpha+\beta=2

now. Similar to (
4.35
)–(
4.37
), we can prove that

\displaystyle\|w(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}+\|z(t)\|_{L^{2}((0,t),W^{1,6})}\leq C\quad{\rm for}\quad t>0.,

(4.40)

and

\displaystyle\|\tilde{f}_{1}(u,v,w,z,)\|_{L^{2}((-\infty,+\infty),L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}+\|\tilde{f}_{2}(u,v,w,z)\|_{L^{2}((-\infty,+\infty),L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))}<+\infty.

(4.41)

Similar to (
4.25
)–(
4.28
) and (
4.38
), denoting
\tilde{u}(t)=e^{-it\Delta}u(t)
and
\tilde{v}(t)=e^{-it\Delta}v(t)
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\|x\tilde{u}(t)-xu_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|x\tilde{v}(t)-xv_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\rightarrow 0\quad{\rm as}\quad t\rightarrow+\infty.

(4.42)

Hence, the solution of (
1.6
) has scattering state in
\Sigma\times\Sigma
.

Therefore, we have proved Theorem 5.
\Box

Remark 4.3.
We cannot establish the
H^{1}\times H^{1}
or
\Sigma\times\Sigma
scattering theory for (
1.6
) when
d=3
and
(\alpha,\beta)
is the endpoint of the critical exponents line
\alpha+\beta=2
, i.e.,
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
or
(\alpha,\beta)=(2,0)
. In the next section, we will establish
\dot{H}^{1}\times\dot{H}^{1}
scattering theory for (
1.6
) with defocusing nonlinearities and
(\alpha,\beta)
is the endpoint on the critical exponents line when
d=3
.

5
\dot{H}^{1}\times\dot{H}^{1}
scattering theory for (
1.6
) with defocusing nonlinearities and
(\alpha,\beta)
is the endpoint on the critical exponents line when
d=3

In this section, we establish the
\dot{H}^{1}\times\dot{H}^{1}
scattering theory for (
1.6
) when
d=3
and
(\alpha,\beta)
is the endpoint on the critical exponents line
\alpha+\beta=2
, i.e.,
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
or
(\alpha,\beta)=(2,0)
.

In the sequels, we only prove Theorem 6 in the case of
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
, the conclusion in the case of
(\alpha,\beta)=(2,0)
can be proved similarly.

We will use the method of reduction to almost periodic solutions and argue by contradiction. We will prove that Theorem 6 holds for solution with small energy by simple contraction mapping arguments. If the conclusions of this theorem are not true, we can find the solution with a transition energy above which the energy no longer controls the spacetime norm. Therefore, first we will show that there is a minimal counterexample which has good compactness properties.

Definition 5.1(Almost periodicity).

A solution
(u,v)\in[L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})]^{2}
of (
1.6
) is said to be almost periodic(modulo symmetries) if there exist functions
\widetilde{N}:I\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
\tilde{x}:I\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and
C:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}

such that for all
t\in I
and
\eta>0

\displaystyle\int_{|x-\tilde{x}(t)|\geq\frac{C(\eta)}{\widetilde{N}(t)}}[|\nabla u(t,x)|^{2}+|\nabla v(t,x)|^{2}]dx+\int_{|\xi|\geq C(\eta)\widetilde{N}(t)}|\xi|^{2}[|\hat{u}(t,\xi)|^{2}+|\hat{v}(t,\xi)|^{2}]d\xi\leq\eta.

(5.1)

The functions
\widetilde{N}(t)
,
\tilde{x}(t)
and
C(\eta)
are called as the frequency scale function for the solution
(u,v)
, the spatial center function and the modulus of compactness respectively.

By compactness, there exists
c(\eta)>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{|x-\tilde{x}(t)|\geq\frac{C(\eta)}{\widetilde{N}(t)}}[|\nabla u(t,x)|^{2}+|\nabla v(t,x)|^{2}]dx+\int_{|\xi|\geq C(\eta)\widetilde{N}(t)}|\xi|^{2}[|\hat{u}(t,\xi)|^{2}+|\hat{v}(t,\xi)|^{2}]d\xi\leq\eta,

(5.2)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|\nabla u(t,x)|^{2}+|\nabla v(t,x)|^{2}]dx\lesssim_{(u,v)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u(t,x)|^{6}+|v(t,x)|^{6}]dx

(5.3)

for all
t\in I
.

The following proposition is very important to the proof of Theorem 6.

Proposition 5.2(Reduction to almost periodic solution).

Assume that Theorem 6 failed. Then there exists a maximal-lifespan solution
(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}

of (
1.6
) with
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
which is almost periodic and blows up both forward and backward in time in the sense of

\int_{t_{0}}^{\sup I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u(t,x)|^{10}+|v(t,x)|^{10}]dxdt=\int^{t_{0}}_{\inf I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}[|u(t,x)|^{10}+|v(t,x)|^{10}]dxdt=+\infty

for all
t_{0}\in I
.

The proof of this proposition will be given in Subsection 5.2 by some conclusions.

By continuity, the modulation parameters
\tilde{x}(t)
and
\widetilde{N}(t)
cannot change rapidly, similar to Lemma 5.18 in
[
60
]
, we obtain

Lemma 5.3(Local constancy property).

Let
(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
be a maximal-lifespan almost period solution of (
1.6
) with
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
. Then there exists a small number
\delta
which depends only on
(u,v)
, such that

\displaystyle[t_{0}-\delta\widetilde{N}(t_{0})^{-2},t_{0}+\delta\widetilde{N}(t_{0})^{-2}]\subset I\quad{\rm if}\quad t_{0}\in I,

(5.4)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}(t)\thicksim_{(u,v)}\widetilde{N}(t_{0})\quad{\rm whenever}\quad|t-t_{0}|\leq\delta\widetilde{N}(t_{0})^{-2}.

(5.5)

As the direct result of (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), we give a corollary of Lemma 5.3 below

Corollary 5.4(
\widetilde{N}(t)
blows up).

Let
(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
be a maximal-lifespan almost period solution of (
1.6
) with
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
. If
T
is any finite endpoint of
I
, then
\widetilde{N}(t)\geq_{(u,v)}|T-t|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Consequently,
\lim_{t\rightarrow T}\tilde{N}(t)=+\infty
.

The following lemma reveals the relation between
\widetilde{N}(t)
of an almost periodic solution
(u,v)
and its Strichartz norms:

Lemma 5.5(Spacetime bounds).

Let
(u,v)
be an almost periodic solution of (
1.6
) with
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
on a time interval
I
. Then for any admissible pair
(q,r)
with
2\leq q<+\infty
,

\displaystyle\int_{I}\widetilde{N}(t)^{2}dt\lesssim_{(u,v)}\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim_{(u,v)}1+\int_{I}\widetilde{N}(t)^{2}dt

(5.6)

Proof:
Similar to the proof of Lemma 5.21 in
[
60
]
, we first prove

\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim_{(u,v)}1+\int_{I}\widetilde{N}(t)^{2}dt.

Let
0<\epsilon<1
be a small parameter to be chosen later and subdivide
I
into
subintervals
I_{j}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{j}}\widetilde{N}^{2}(t)dt\leq\epsilon,

(5.7)

the number of such intervals is at most
\epsilon^{-1}[1+\int_{I}\widetilde{N}(t)^{2}dt]
.

For each
j
, we may choose
t_{j}\in I_{j}
such that

\displaystyle|I_{j}|^{\frac{d-2}{2(d+2)}}[\widetilde{N}^{2}(t_{j})]^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}\leq 2\epsilon.

(5.8)

Using Strichartz estimate, for any admissible pair
(q,r)
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}+\|\nabla v\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{(u,v)}\|\nabla u_{\geq M\widetilde{N}^{2}(t_{j})}(t_{j})\|^{q}_{L^{2}_{x}}+|I_{j}|^{\frac{q(d-2)}{2(d+2)}}[M\widetilde{N}^{2}(t_{j})]^{\frac{2qd}{d+2}}\|\nabla u(t_{j})\|^{q}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla v_{\geq M\widetilde{N}^{2}(t_{j})}(t_{j})\|^{q}_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad+|I_{j}|^{\frac{q(d-2)}{2(d+2)}}[M\widetilde{N}^{2}(t_{j})]^{\frac{2qd}{d+2}}\|\nabla v(t_{j})\|^{q}_{L^{2}_{x}}+[\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}+\|\nabla v\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}]^{\frac{(d+2)}{d}},

where all spacetime norms are taken on the slab
I_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. If
M
is large, by Definition 5.1, the first term can be small. By (
5.8
), the second term also can small. Hence, we can use the bootstrap argument to get

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}+\|\nabla v\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim_{(u,v)}1+\int_{I}\widetilde{N}(t)^{2}dt.

(5.9)

On the other hand, since
(u,v)\not\equiv(0,0)
,
[\widetilde{N}(t)]^{-\frac{2}{q}}[\|\nabla u\|_{L^{r}_{x}}+\|\nabla v\|_{L^{r}_{x}}]
will never vanish, almost periodicity implies that it is bounded away from zero, i.e.,

[\|\nabla u\|_{L^{r}_{x}}+\|\nabla v\|_{L^{r}_{x}}]\gtrsim_{(u,v)}[\widetilde{N}(t)]^{\frac{2}{q}}.

Integrating it on
I
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{I}\widetilde{N}(t)^{2}dt\lesssim_{(u,v)}\|\nabla u\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v\|^{q}_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

(5.10)

(
6.3
) is proved.
\Box

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\widetilde{N}(t)\geq 1
on
[0,T_{\max})
because it can be realized by a simple rescaling argument. Finally, we get

Proposition 5.6(Two special scenarios for blowup).

Assume that Theorem 6 failed. Then there exists an almost periodic solution
(u,v):[0,T_{\max})\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
such that

\|u\|_{L_{t,x}^{10}([0,T_{\max})\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v\|_{L_{t,x}^{10}([0,T_{\max})\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}=+\infty

and
[0,T_{\max})=\cup_{k}J_{k}
, where
J_{k}
are characteristic intervals on which
\widetilde{N}(t)\equiv N_{k}\geq 1
.
Moreover,

{\rm either}\quad\int_{0}^{T_{\max}}\widetilde{N}(t)^{-1}dt<+\infty\quad{\rm or}\quad\int_{0}^{T_{\max}}\widetilde{N}(t)^{-1}dt=+\infty.

Consequently, we only need to preclude the existence of the two types of almost periodic solution described in Proposition 5.6, then we can give the proof of Theorem 6. The proof of Proposition 5.6 will be given later.

To prove the no-existence of cascade solutions, we need the following proposition.

Proposition 5.7(No-waste Duhamel formulae).

Let
(u,v):[0,T_{\max})\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
be defined as in Proposition 5.6. Then for all
t\in[0,T_{\max})

\displaystyle u(t)=i\lim_{T\rightarrow T_{\max}}\int_{t}^{T}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}|v|^{4}u(s)ds,\quad v(t)=i\lim_{T\rightarrow T_{\max}}\int_{t}^{T}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}|u|^{2}|v|^{2}v(s)ds

(5.11)

in the weak
\dot{H}^{1}_{x}
topology.

Proof:
If
T_{\max}<+\infty
, then by Corollary 5.4,

\lim_{t\rightarrow T_{\max}}\widetilde{N}(t)=+\infty.

By Definition 5.1, this implies that
(u(t),v(t))
converges weakly to
(0,0)
as
t\rightarrow T_{\max}
. Since

T_{\max}<+\infty
and the map
t\rightarrow e^{-it\Delta}
is continuous in the strong operator topology on

L^{2}_{x}
, we know that
(e^{-it\Delta}u(t),e^{-it\Delta}v(t))
converges weakly to
(0,0)
.

If
T_{\max}=+\infty
, then for any test functions pair
(\varphi,\psi)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\eta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\quad|<u(t),e^{it\Delta}\varphi>_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}|+|<v(t),e^{it\Delta}\psi>_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{|x-x(t)|\leq\frac{C(\eta)}{\widetilde{N}(t)}}|e^{it\Delta}\varphi|^{2}dx+\eta\|\varphi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\int_{|x-x(t)|\leq\frac{C(\eta)}{\widetilde{N}(t)}}|e^{it\Delta}\psi|^{2}dx+\eta\|\psi\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}.

(5.12)

Using Fraunhofer formula, by the results of Corollary 5.4 and changing variables, we can see that
(e^{-it\Delta}u(t),e^{-it\Delta}v(t))
converges weakly to
(0,0)
.

In any case,
(e^{-it\Delta}u(t),e^{-it\Delta}v(t))
converges weakly to
(0,0)
as
t\rightarrow T_{\max}
, then we can use Duhamel formulae to get (
5.11
).
\Box

5.1
Stability result about (
1.6
)

Since we have established the local wellposedness of
H^{1}\times H^{1}
-solution to (
1.6
) with initial data
(u_{0},v_{0})\in H^{1}\times H^{1}
, to remove the constraint conditions
(u_{0},v_{0})\in L^{2}\times L^{2}
, in this subsection, we show a stability result about (
1.6
) when
d=3
and
(\alpha,\beta)
is the endpoint of critical exponents line, which will prove the local wellposedness of
\dot{H}^{1}\times\dot{H}^{1}
-solution to (
1.6
) with initial data
(u_{0},v_{0})\in\dot{H}^{1}\times\dot{H}^{1}
. Since there is the similar stability result about (
1.6
) when
d=4
and
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,0)
, we would like to put them together and state below. Although the proofs of some propositions and lemmas in this subsection are very similar to those in
[
60
]
for the scalar equation and seem standard, however, to our best knowledge, the present paper is the first one discussing
\dot{H}^{1}\times\dot{H}^{1}
scattering phenomenon on a coupled system of Schrödinger equations in energy critical case, for completeness, we would like to give the details.

Proposition 5.8(Stability result).

Let
I
be a compact time interval
and let
(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})
be an approximate solution of (
1.6
) on
I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense of

\displaystyle i\tilde{u}_{t}+\Delta\tilde{u}=\lambda|\tilde{u}|^{\alpha}|\tilde{v}|^{\beta+2}\tilde{u}+e_{1},\quad i\tilde{v}_{t}+\Delta\tilde{v}=\mu|\tilde{u}|^{\alpha+2}|\tilde{v}|^{\beta}\tilde{u}+e_{2}.

(5.13)

for some functions
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
. Here
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
or
(\alpha,\beta)=(2,0)
when
d=3
, while
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,0)
when
d=4
. Suppose that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}\leq E,\quad\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\leq L

(5.14)

for some positive constants
E
and
L
. Assume that
t_{0}\in I
and

\displaystyle\|u(t_{0})-\tilde{u}(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|v(t_{0})-\tilde{v}(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}\leq E^{\prime},

(5.15)

\displaystyle\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}[u(t_{0})-\tilde{u}(t_{0})]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}[v(t_{0})-\tilde{v}(t_{0})]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\leq\epsilon,

(5.16)

\displaystyle\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}(I)}\leq\epsilon

(5.17)

for some positive constants
E^{\prime}
and
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{1}(E,E^{\prime},L)
. Then there exists a unique strong solution

(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
with initial data
(u(t_{0}),v(t_{0}))
at
t_{0}
which satisfies

\displaystyle\|\nabla u(t)]\|_{\dot{S}^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla v]\|_{\dot{S}^{0}(I)}\leq C(E,E^{\prime},L),

(5.18)

\displaystyle\|\nabla[u(t)-\tilde{u}(t)]\|_{\dot{S}^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla[v(t)-\tilde{v}(t)]\|_{\dot{S}^{0}(I)}\leq C(E,E^{\prime},L)E^{\prime},

(5.19)

\displaystyle\|[u(t)-\tilde{u}(t)]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|[v(t)-\tilde{v}(t)]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\leq C(E,E^{\prime},L)\epsilon^{c}

(5.20)

for some
0<c=c(d)<1
.

As a consequences of Proposition 5.8, we have the following local well-posedness result.

Corollary 5.9(Local well-posednes).

Let
I
be a compact time interval,
t_{0}\in I
. Assume that
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
or
(\alpha,\beta)=(2,0)
when
d=3
, while
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,0)
when
d=4
,
(u_{0},v_{0})\in\dot{H}^{1}\times\dot{H}^{1}
and

\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|v_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}\leq E.

For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\delta=\delta(E,\epsilon)
such that if

\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}u(t_{0})\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}v(t_{0})\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\leq\delta,

then (
1.6
) has a unique solution
(u,v)
with initial data
(u(t_{0}),v(t_{0}))=(u(0),v(0))
and

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|v(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\epsilon,\quad\|\nabla u\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla v\|_{S^{0}(I)}\leq 2E.

In order to prove stability Theorem, similar to
[
60
]
, for any time interval
I
, we introduce some spaces
X^{0}(I)
,
X(I)
,
Y(I)
and denote

\displaystyle\|w\|_{X^{0}(I)}=\|w\|_{L^{\frac{d(d+2)}{2(d-2)}}_{t}L^{\frac{2d^{2}(d+2)}{(d+4)(d-2)^{2}}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(5.21)

\displaystyle\|w\|_{X(I)}=\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{d+2}}w\|_{L^{\frac{d(d+2)}{2(d-2)}}_{t}L^{\frac{2d^{2}(d+2)}{d^{3}-4d+16}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(5.22)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{Y(I)}=\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{d+2}}f\|_{L^{\frac{d}{2}}_{t}L^{\frac{2d^{2}(d+2)}{d^{3}+4d^{2}+4d-16}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(5.23)

Then by the results of Lemma 3.10 and Lemma 3.11 in
[
60
]
,

\displaystyle\|w\|_{X^{0}(I)}\lesssim\|w\|_{X(I)}\lesssim\|\nabla w\|_{S^{0}(I)},

(5.24)

\displaystyle\|w\|_{X(I)}\lesssim\|w\|^{\frac{1}{d+2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\nabla w\|^{\frac{d+1}{d+2}}_{S^{0}(I)},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|w\|^{c}_{X(I)}\|\nabla w\|^{1-c}_{S^{0}(I)}\quad{\rm for\ some}\ 0<c=c(d)\leq 1,

(5.26)

\displaystyle\|\int^{t}_{t_{0}}e^{i(t-s)\Delta}f(s)\|_{X(I)}\lesssim\|f\|_{Y(I)}.

(5.27)

Now we will prove that

Lemma 5.10(Nonlinear estimates).

Denote
f_{1}(u,v)=\lambda|v|^{4}u
and
f_{2}(u,v)=\mu|u|^{2}|v|^{2}v
if
d=3
, while
f_{1}(u,v)=\lambda|v|^{2}u
and
f_{2}(u,v)=\mu|u|^{2}v
if
d=4
. Let
I
be a time interval. Then

\displaystyle\|f_{1}(u,v)\|_{Y(I)}+\|f_{2}(u,v)\|_{Y(I)}\lesssim\|u\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{X(I)}+\|v\|^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}_{X(I)},

(5.28)

\displaystyle\|f_{1u}(u_{1}+u_{2},v_{1}+v_{2})w_{1}\|_{Y(I)}+\|f_{1\bar{u}}(u_{1}+u_{2},v_{1}+v_{2})\bar{w}_{1}\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\ +\|f_{1v}(u_{1}+u_{2},v_{1}+v_{2})w_{2}\|_{Y(I)}+\|f_{1\bar{v}}(u_{1}+u_{2},v_{1}+v_{2})\bar{w}_{2}\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\ +\|f_{2u}(u_{1}+u_{2},v_{1}+v_{2})w_{1}\|_{Y(I)}+\|f_{2\bar{u}}(u_{1}+u_{2},v_{1}+v_{2})\bar{w}_{1}\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\ +\|f_{2v}(u_{1}+u_{2},v_{1}+v_{2})w_{2}\|_{Y(I)}+\|f_{2\bar{v}}(u_{1}+u_{2},v_{1}+v_{2})\bar{w}_{2}\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{m=1}^{2}[\|u_{m}\|^{\frac{8}{d^{2}-4}}_{X(I)}\|\nabla u_{m}\|^{\frac{4d}{d^{2}-4}}_{S^{0}(I)}+\|v_{m}\|^{\frac{8}{d^{2}-4}}_{X(I)}\|\nabla v_{m}\|^{\frac{4d}{d^{2}-4}}_{S^{0}(I)}]\right)[\|w_{1}\|_{X(I)}+\|w_{2}\|_{X(I)}].

(5.29)

Proof:
If
d=3
, then

\displaystyle\|w\|_{X^{0}(I)}=\|w\|_{L^{\frac{15}{2}}_{t}L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})},\quad\|w\|_{X(I)}=\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}w\|_{L^{\frac{15}{2}}_{t}L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})},

\displaystyle\|f\|_{Y(I)}=\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}f\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}L^{\frac{90}{59}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Using Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, and the interpolation (
5.24
)–(
5.27
), we have

\displaystyle\quad\|f_{1}(u,v)\|_{Y(I)}=\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}(|v|^{4}u)\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}L^{\frac{90}{59}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\|u\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}(|v|^{4})\|_{L^{\frac{90}{52}}_{x}}+\||v|^{4}\|_{L^{\frac{90}{28}}_{x}}\|\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}u\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}]\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\|u\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|(|v|^{3})\|_{L^{\frac{30}{7}}_{x}}\|\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}v\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}+\|v\|^{4}_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}u\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}]\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\|u\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|v\|^{3}_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}v\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}+\|v\|^{4}_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}u\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}]\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{X^{0}(I)}\|v\|^{3}_{X^{0}(I)}\|v\|_{X(I)}+\|v\|^{4}_{X^{0}(I)}\|u\|_{X(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|^{5}_{X(I)}+\|v\|^{5}_{X(I)},

(5.30)

\displaystyle\quad\|f_{2}(u,v)\|_{Y(I)}=\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}(|u|^{2}|v|^{2}v)|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}L^{\frac{90}{59}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\||u|^{2}\|_{L^{\frac{45}{7}}_{x}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}|v|^{2}v\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}(|u|^{2})\|_{L^{\frac{90}{38}}_{x}}\||v|^{2}v\|_{L^{\frac{30}{7}}_{x}}]\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\|u\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|v\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}v\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}+\|u\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}u\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}\|v\|^{3}_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}]\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|^{2}_{X^{0}(I)}\|v\|^{2}_{X^{0}(I)}\|v\|_{X(I)}+\|u\|_{X^{0}(I)}\|u\|_{X(I)}\|v\|^{3}_{X^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|^{5}_{X(I)}+\|v\|^{5}_{X(I)}.

(5.31)

(
5.28
) is proved when
d=3
.

Since

\displaystyle f_{1u}=\lambda|v|^{4},\qquad f_{1\bar{u}}=0,\qquad\quad f_{1v}=2\lambda|v|^{2}\bar{v}u,\quad f_{1\bar{v}}=2\lambda|v|^{2}vu,

\displaystyle f_{2u}=\mu|v|^{2}\bar{u}v,\quad f_{2\bar{u}}=\mu|v|^{2}uv,\quad f_{2v}=\mu|u|^{2}|v|^{2},\quad f_{2\bar{v}}=\mu|u|^{2}v^{2},

\displaystyle LHS(\ref{9191})

\displaystyle\lesssim\||v_{1}+v_{2}|^{4}w_{1}\|_{Y(I)}+\||v_{1}+v_{2}|^{2}(\bar{v}_{1}+\bar{v}_{2})(u_{1}+u_{2})w_{2}\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\||v_{1}+v_{2}|^{2}(u_{1}+u_{2})(v_{1}+v_{2})\bar{w}_{2}\|_{Y(I)}+\||v_{1}+v_{2}|^{2}(\bar{u}_{1}+\bar{u}_{2})(v_{1}+v_{2})w_{1}\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\||v_{1}+v_{2}|^{2}(u_{1}+u_{2})(v_{1}+v_{2})w_{1}\|_{Y(I)}+\||u_{1}+u_{2}|^{2}|v_{1}+v_{2}|^{2}w_{2}\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\||u_{1}+u_{2}|^{2}(v_{1}+v_{2})^{2}w_{2}\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle:=(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)+(V)+(VI)+(VII).

(5.32)

We just give the details of estimating (IV) below,

\displaystyle(IV)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\||v_{1}+v_{2}|^{2}(\bar{u}_{1}+\bar{u}_{2})(v_{1}+v_{2})\|_{L^{\frac{45}{14}}_{x}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}w_{1}\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}

\displaystyle\quad+\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}(|v_{1}+v_{2}|^{2}(\bar{u}_{1}+\bar{u}_{2})(v_{1}+v_{2}))\|_{L^{\frac{45}{26}}_{x}}\|w_{1}\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}]\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\left(\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|^{3}_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|u_{1}+u_{2}\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}w_{1}\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}\right)\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\left(\||v_{1}+v_{2}|^{2}(v_{1}+v_{2})\|_{L^{\frac{30}{7}}_{x}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}(\bar{u}_{1}+\bar{u}_{2})\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}\|w_{1}\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\right)\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\left(\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\||\nabla|^{\frac{4}{5}}(v_{1}+v_{2})\|_{L^{\frac{90}{31}}_{x}}\|\bar{u}_{1}+\bar{u}_{2}\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\|w_{1}\|_{L^{\frac{90}{7}}_{x}}\right)\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|^{3}_{X^{0}(I)}\|u_{1}+u_{2}\|_{X(I)}\|w_{1}\|_{X^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|^{2}_{X^{0}(I)}\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|_{X(I)}\|u_{1}+u_{2}\|_{X^{0}(I)}\|w_{1}\|_{X^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\|u_{1}+u_{2}\|^{\frac{8}{5}}_{X(I)}\|\nabla(u_{1}+u_{2})\|^{\frac{12}{5}}_{S^{0}(I)}+\|v_{1}+v_{2}\|^{\frac{8}{5}}_{X(I)}\|\nabla(v_{1}+v_{2})\|^{\frac{12}{5}}_{S^{0}(I)}\right]\|w_{1}\|_{X(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim RHS(\ref{9191}).

(5.33)

Other terms can be estimated similarly, just using Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality and (
5.24
). Hence (
5.29
) is proved when
d=3
.

If
d=4
,

\displaystyle\|w\|_{X^{0}(I)}=\|w\|_{L^{6}_{t}L^{6}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{4})},\ \|w\|_{X(I)}=\||\nabla|^{\frac{2}{3}}w\|_{L^{6}_{t}L^{3}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{4})},\ \|f\|_{Y(I)}=\||\nabla|^{\frac{2}{3}}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\frac{3}{2}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{4})}.

Similar to (
5.31
), we can use Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, and the interpolation (
5.24
)–(
5.27
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{1}(u,v)\|_{Y(I)}\lesssim\|u\|^{3}_{X(I)}+\|v\|^{3}_{X(I)}.

(5.34)

Similar to (
5.32
) and (
5.33
), we can prove (
5.29
) when
d=4
, we omit the details here.

Putting the results above, Lemma 5.10 is proved.
\Box

To prove the stability result, we still need the following short-time perturbation result.

Lemma 5.11(Short-time perturbation).

Denote
f_{1}(u,v)=\lambda|v|^{4}u
and
f_{2}(u,v)=\mu|u|^{2}|v|^{2}v
if
d=3
, while
f_{1}(u,v)=\lambda|v|^{2}u
and
f_{2}(u,v)=\mu|u|^{2}v
if
d=4
. Let
I
be a compact time interval and
(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})
be an approximate solution of (
1.6
) on
I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
in the sense that

\displaystyle i\tilde{u}_{t}+\Delta\tilde{u}=f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})+e_{1},\quad i\tilde{v}_{t}+\Delta\tilde{v}=f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})+e_{2}

for some
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
. Suppose that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq E

for some
E
. Let
t_{0}\in I
and
(u(t_{0}),v(t_{0}))
satisfy

\|u(t_{0})-\tilde{u}(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|v(t_{0})-\tilde{v}(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}\leq E^{\prime}

for some
E^{\prime}
. Assume that there exist some small
0<\delta=\delta(E)
and
0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}(E,E^{\prime})
such that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}\|_{X(I)}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{X(I)}\leq\delta,

(5.35)

\displaystyle\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}[u(t_{0})-\tilde{u}(t_{0})]\|_{X(I)}+\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}[v(t_{0})-\tilde{v}(t_{0})]\|_{X(I)}\leq\epsilon,

(5.36)

\displaystyle\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}(I)}\leq\epsilon.

(5.37)

Then there exists a unique solution
(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
of (
1.6
) with initial data
(u(t_{0}),v(t_{0}))

at time
t=t_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{X(I)}+\|v-\tilde{v}\|_{X(I)}\lesssim\epsilon,

(5.38)

\displaystyle\|\nabla(u-\tilde{u})\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla(v-\tilde{v})\|_{S^{0}(I)}\lesssim E^{\prime},

(5.39)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla v\|_{S^{0}(I)}\lesssim E+E^{\prime},

(5.40)

\displaystyle\|f_{1}(u,v)-f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{Y(I)}+\|f_{2}(u,v)-f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{Y(I)}\lesssim\epsilon,

(5.41)

\displaystyle\|\nabla[f_{1}(u,v)-f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})]\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla[f_{2}(u,v)-f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})]\|_{N^{0}(I)}\lesssim E^{\prime}.

(5.42)

Proof:
Inspired by the proof of Lemma 3.13 in
[
60
]
, we prove it under the additional assumption
M(u)+M(v)<+\infty
,
which can guarantee that
(u,v)
exists by the result of local well-posedness of
H^{1}\times H^{1}
-solution. The additional assumption

M(u)+M(v)<+\infty
can be removed a posteriori by the usual limiting argument: Approximating

(u(t_{0}),v(t_{0}))
in
\dot{H}^{1}_{x}\times\dot{H}^{1}_{x}
by a sequence
\{(u_{n}(t_{0}),v_{n}(t_{0}))\}_{n}\subset H^{1}_{x}\times H^{1}_{x}
, and letting

(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})=(u_{m},v_{m})
,
(u,v)=(u_{n},v_{n})
and
e_{1}=e_{2}=0
, we can prove that the sequence of solutions
\{(u_{n},v_{n})\}_{n}

with initial data
\{(u_{n}(t_{0}),v_{n}(t_{0}))\}_{n}

is a Cauchy sequence in energy-critical norms and convergent to a solution
(u,v)
with initial
data
(u(t_{0}),v(t_{0}))
which satisfies
(\nabla u,\nabla v)\in S^{0}(I)\times S^{0}(I)
. Therefore, we assume that the solution
(u,v)
exists and
(\nabla u,\nabla v)\in S^{0}(I)\times S^{0}(I)
, we only to prove (
5.38
)–(
5.41
) as a priori estimates.

First, we estimate some bounds on
(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})
and
(u,v)
. Using Strichartz, interpolation inequalities (
5.24
)–(
5.27
), the assumptions (
5.35
) and (
5.37
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim E+[\|\tilde{u}\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|\tilde{v}\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}][\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I)}]+\epsilon

\displaystyle\lesssim E+\delta^{\frac{4c}{d-2}}[\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|^{1+\frac{4(1-c)}{d-2}}_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|^{1+\frac{4(1-c)}{d-2}}_{S^{0}(I)}]+\epsilon.

Here
c=c(d)
is the same as that in interpolation inequalities (
5.24
)–(
5.27
). Choosing
\delta
small depending on
d
and
E
,
\epsilon_{0}
small enough depending on
E
, we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I)}\lesssim E.

(5.43)

Meanwhile, by Strichartz estimates, nonlinear estimates, (
5.35
) and (
5.37
), if
\delta
and
\epsilon
are chosen small enough, then

\displaystyle\quad\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}\tilde{u}(t_{0})\|_{X(I)}+\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}\tilde{v}(t_{0})\|_{X(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{u}\|_{X(I)}+\|f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{Y(I)}+\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\tilde{v}\|_{X(I)}+\|f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{Y(I)}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta+\delta^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}+\epsilon.

Recalling (
5.36
), we have

\displaystyle\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}u(t_{0})\|_{X(I)}+\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}v(t_{0})\|_{X(I)}\lesssim\delta.

Using interpolation inequalities (
5.24
)–(
5.27
) and nonlinear estimates again, we get

\displaystyle\|u\|_{X(I)}+\|v\|_{X(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}u(t_{0})\|_{X(I)}+\|f_{1}(u,v)\|_{Y(I)}+\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}v(t_{0})\|_{X(I)}+\|f_{2}(u,v)\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta+[\|u\|_{X(I)}+\|v\|_{X(I)}]^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}.

Choosing
\delta
small enough, by the usual bootstrap argument, we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{X(I)}+\|v\|_{X(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta.

(5.44)

Next, let
(w,z):=(u-\tilde{u},v-\tilde{v})
. Then

\displaystyle iw_{t}+\Delta w=f_{1}(\tilde{u}+w,\tilde{v}+z)-f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})-e_{1},

\displaystyle iz_{t}+\Delta z=f_{2}(\tilde{u}+w,\tilde{v}+z)-f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})-e_{2},

\displaystyle w(t_{0})=u(t_{0})-\tilde{u}(t_{0}),\quad z(t_{0})=v(t_{0})-\tilde{v}(t_{0}).

Using Strichartz estimates, interpolation inequalities, nonlinear estimates and (
5.37
), we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\|w\|_{X(I)}+\|z\|_{X(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{it\Delta}[u(t_{0})-\tilde{u}(t_{0})]\|_{X(I)}+\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|f_{1}(\tilde{u}+w,\tilde{v}+z)-f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|e^{it\Delta}[v(t_{0})-\tilde{v}(t_{0})]\|_{X(I)}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|f_{2}(\tilde{u}+w,\tilde{v}+z)-f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{Y(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon+\delta^{\frac{8}{d^{2}-4}}E^{\frac{4d}{d^{2}-4}}[\|w\|_{X(I)}+\|z\|_{X(I)}]+[\|\nabla w\|_{S^{0}(I)}^{\frac{4d}{d^{2}-4}}+\|\nabla z\|_{S^{0}(I)}^{\frac{4d}{d^{2}-4}}][\|w\|^{\frac{d^{2}+4}{d^{2}-4}}_{X(I)}+\|z\|^{\frac{d^{2}+4}{d^{2}-4}}_{X(I)}].

(5.45)

Using Strichartz estimate, nonlinear estimates, Hölder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\quad\|\nabla w\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla z\|_{S^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u(t_{0})-\tilde{u}(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|f_{1}(\tilde{u}+w,\tilde{v}+z)-f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{N^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|v(t_{0})-\tilde{v}(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|f_{2}(\tilde{u}+w,\tilde{v}+z)-f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{N^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim E^{\prime}+\epsilon+[\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I)}][\|u\|_{X^{0}(I)}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{X^{0}(I)}]^{\frac{6-d}{d-2}}[\|w\|_{X^{0}(I)}+\|z\|_{X^{0}(I)}]

\displaystyle\quad+[\|u\|_{X^{0}(I)}+\|v\|_{X^{0}(I)}]^{\frac{4}{d-2}}[\|\nabla w\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla z\|_{S^{0}(I)}]

\displaystyle\lesssim E^{\prime}+\epsilon+[E\delta^{\frac{6-d}{d-2}}+\delta^{\frac{4}{d-2}}][\|\nabla w\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla z\|_{S^{0}(I)}].

(5.46)

Combining (
5.45
) with (
5.46
), we obtain (
5.38
) and (
5.39
). (
5.39
) and (
5.43
) imply (
5.40
).
(
5.38
), (
5.39
), (
5.45
) and (
5.46
) deduce (
5.41
) and (
5.42
).
\Box

Now we give the proof of stability result below.

Proof of Proposition 5.8:
First, we show that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I)}\leq C(E,L).

(5.47)

Under the assumption (
5.14
), we can divide
I
into
J_{0}=J(L,\eta)
subintervals
I_{j}=(t_{j},t_{j+1})
such that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}\leq\eta

for some small
\eta>0
to be chosen later on each spacetime slab
I_{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Using Strichartz estimate and (
5.17
),
we have

\displaystyle\quad\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I_{j})}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I_{j})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{u}(t_{j})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{N^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\tilde{v}(t_{j})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|\nabla f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{N^{0}(I)}

\displaystyle\lesssim E+\epsilon+[\|\tilde{u}\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}+\|\tilde{v}\|^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}][\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I_{j})}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I_{j})}]

\displaystyle\lesssim E+\epsilon+\eta^{\frac{4}{d-2}}_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}}[\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I_{j})}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I_{j})}].

If we choose
\eta
and
\epsilon
small enough depending on
E
, we can obtain

\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{S^{0}(I_{j})}+\|\nabla\tilde{v}\|_{S^{0}(I_{j})}\lesssim E.

Sum them over all
I_{j}
, we get (
5.47
).

By interpolation inequalities (
5.24
)–(
5.27
) and (
5.47
), under the assumptions (
5.15
)–(
5.16
),
we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}\|_{X(I)}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{X(I)}\leq C(E,L),

(5.48)

\displaystyle\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}[u(t_{0})-\tilde{u}(t_{0})]\|_{X(I)}+\|e^{i(t-t_{0})\Delta}[v(t_{0})-\tilde{v}(t_{0})]\|_{X(I)}\lesssim\epsilon^{\frac{1}{d+2}}(E^{\prime})^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}.

(5.49)

(
5.47
) implies that we can divide
I
into
J_{1}=J_{1}(E,L)
subintervals
I_{j}=[t_{j},t_{j+1}]
such that

\|\tilde{u}\|_{X(I_{j})}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{X(I_{j})}\lesssim\delta

for the same small
\delta=\delta(E)
in (
5.35
), while (
5.49
) can guarantee (
5.36
) if we take
\epsilon
small enough.

By Strichartz estimate and the inductive hypothesis,

\displaystyle\quad\|e^{i(t-t_{j})\Delta}[u(t_{j})-\tilde{u}(t_{j})]\|_{X(I_{j})}+\|e^{i(t-t_{j})\Delta}[v(t_{j})-\tilde{v}(t_{j})]\|_{X(I_{j})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{i(t-t_{j})\Delta}[u(t_{j})-\tilde{u}(t_{j})]\|_{X(I_{j})}+\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|f_{1}(u,v)-f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{Y([t_{0},t_{j}])}

\displaystyle\quad+\|e^{i(t-t_{j})\Delta}[v(t_{j})-\tilde{v}(t_{j})]\|_{X(I_{j})}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}(I)}+\|f_{2}(u,v)-f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{Y([t_{0},t_{j}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon^{c}+\epsilon+\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}C(k)\epsilon^{c},

\displaystyle\quad\|(u(t_{j})-\tilde{u}(t_{j}))\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|(v(t_{j})-\tilde{v}(t_{j}))\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u(t_{j})-\tilde{u}(t_{j}))\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla e_{1}\|_{N^{0}([t_{0},t_{j}])}+\|f_{1}(u,v)-f_{1}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{N^{0}([t_{0},t_{j}])}

\displaystyle\quad+\|(v(t_{j})-\tilde{v}(t_{j}))\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}}+\|\nabla e_{2}\|_{N^{0}([t_{0},t_{j}])}+\|f_{2}(u,v)-f_{2}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\|_{N^{0}([t_{0},t_{j}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim E^{\prime}+\epsilon+\sum_{k=0}^{j-1}C(k)E^{\prime}.

Summing the bounds over all subintervals
I_{j}
, and using the interpolation inequalities (
5.24
)–(
5.27
), we can obtain (
5.38
)–(
5.42
) and prove Proposition 5.8.
\Box

5.2
Concentration compactness and reduction to almost periodic solution

In this subsection, we first give the linear profile decomposition which will lead to the reduction to almost periodic solution.

First we give the symmetry group for a pair of functions.

Definition 5.12(Symmetry group)

(i) For any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}/2\pi\mathbb{Z}
, position
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and scaling parameter
\tilde{\lambda}>0
, we define a unitary transformation

g_{\theta,x_{0},\tilde{\lambda}}(\varphi,\psi):\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

by

[g_{\theta,x_{0},\tilde{\lambda}}(\varphi,\psi)](x):=\tilde{\lambda}^{-\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta+2}}e^{i\theta}(\varphi(\frac{x-x_{0}}{\tilde{\lambda}}),\psi(\frac{x-x_{0}}{\tilde{\lambda}}));

(ii) Denote the collection of such transformations in (i) by
G
;

(iii) For any
(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
, we define
T_{\theta,x_{0},\tilde{\lambda}}(u,v):\tilde{\lambda}^{2}I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
by

[T_{\theta,x_{0},\tilde{\lambda}}(u,v)](x):=\tilde{\lambda}^{-\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta+2}}e^{i\theta}(u(\frac{t}{\tilde{\lambda}^{2}},\frac{x-x_{0}}{\tilde{\lambda}}),v(\frac{t}{\tilde{\lambda}^{2}},\frac{x-x_{0}}{\tilde{\lambda}})).

We state the the linear profile decomposition below, which was proved in
[
49
,
76
]
.

Lemma 5.13(Linear profile decomposition).

Let
(u_{n},v_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of function pairs bounded in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
d\geq 3
. Then after passing to a subsequence if necessary, there exist a sequence of group elements
\{g^{j}_{n}\}_{j\geq 1,n\geq 1}\in G
, times
\{t^{j}_{n}\}_{j\geq 1,n\geq 1}\subset\mathbb{R}
and function pairs
\{(\varphi^{j},\psi^{j})\}_{j\geq 1}\subset\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for all
J\geq 1
, the following decomposition is true

\displaystyle u_{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}g_{n}^{j}e^{it_{n}^{j}\Delta}\phi_{1}^{j}+\varphi_{n}^{J},\quad v_{n}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}g_{n}^{j}e^{it_{n}^{j}\Delta}\phi_{2}^{j}+\psi_{n}^{J}.

Moreover, the following properties hold:

(i)
(\varphi_{n}^{J},\psi_{n}^{J})\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\displaystyle\lim_{J\rightarrow+\infty}\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\left(\|e^{it\Delta}\varphi_{n}^{J}\|_{L^{\frac{(\alpha+\beta+2)(d+2)}{2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|e^{it\Delta}\psi_{n}^{J}\|_{L^{\frac{(\alpha+\beta+2)(d+2)}{2}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\right)=0.

(ii) For any
j\neq j^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j^{\prime}}}+\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j^{\prime}}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j}}+\frac{|x_{n}^{j}-x_{n}^{j^{\prime}}|^{2}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j}\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j^{\prime}}}+\frac{|t_{n}^{j}(\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j})^{2}-t_{n}^{j^{\prime}}(\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j^{\prime}})^{2}|}{\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j}\tilde{\lambda}_{n}^{j^{\prime}}}\rightarrow+\infty\quad{\rm as}\quad n\rightarrow+\infty.

(iii) For any
J\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left(\|\nabla u_{n}\|_{2}^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\|\nabla\phi_{1}^{j}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\nabla\varphi_{n}^{J}\|_{2}^{2}\right)=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left(\|\nabla v_{n}\|_{2}^{2}-\sum_{j=1}^{J}\|\nabla\phi_{2}^{j}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\nabla\psi_{n}^{J}\|_{2}^{2}\right)=0,

and for any
1\leq j\leq J
,

\displaystyle e^{-t_{n}^{j}\Delta}\left((g_{n}^{j})^{-1}(\varphi_{n}^{J},\psi_{n}^{J})\right)\rightarrow(0,0)\quad{\rm weakly\ in}\quad\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad{\rm as}\quad n\rightarrow+\infty.

We will prove the existence of a minimal kinetic energy blowup solution to (
1.6
) below. Our idea borrows from
[
43
,
55
,
60
]
.

Define

\displaystyle S_{I}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\int_{I}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[|u(t,x)|^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}+|v(t,x)|^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}]dxdt,

(5.50)

\displaystyle L^{+}(E_{0})

\displaystyle=\sup\{S_{I}(u,v)|(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C},\ s.t.\ \sup_{t\in I}[\|\nabla u(t)\|^{2}_{2}+\|\nabla v(t)\|^{2}_{2}]=E_{0}\},

(5.51)

where the supremum is taken over all solutions
(u,v)
of (
1.6
),
L^{+}(E_{0}):[0,+\infty)\rightarrow[0,+\infty)
is non-decreasing and

L^{+}(E_{0})\lesssim E_{0}^{\frac{d+2}{d-2}}
for
E_{0}\leq\eta_{0}
.

By the stability result,
L^{+}
is continuous, and there exists a unique critical kinetic energy
0<E_{c}\leq+\infty
such that
L^{+}(E_{0})<+\infty
for
E_{0}<E_{c}
and

L^{+}(E_{0})=+\infty
for
E_{0}\geq E_{c}
.

If
(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
is a maximal-lifespan solution of (
1.6
) such that

\sup_{t\in I}[\|\nabla u\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla v\|_{2}^{2}]<E^{c},

then
(u,v)
is global and

S_{\mathbb{R}}(u,v)\leq L(\sup_{t\in I}[\|\nabla u\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla v\|_{2}^{2}]).

We can get the following key compactness result.

Lemma 5.14(Palais-Smale condition modulo symmetries).

Assume that
d=3
or
d=4
. Let
(u_{n},v_{n}):I_{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
be a sequence of solutions of (
1.6
) with
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
or
(\alpha,\beta)=(2,0)
when
d=3
and
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,0)
when
d=4
, satisfying

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\sup_{t\in I_{n}}[\|\nabla u_{n}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla v_{n}\|_{2}^{2}]=E_{c}

(5.52)

and there exist some sequence of times
t_{n}\in I_{n}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}S_{\geq t_{n}}(u_{n},v_{n})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}S_{\leq t_{n}}(u_{n},v_{n})=+\infty.

Then the sequence
(u_{n}(t_{n}),v_{n}(t_{n}))
has a subsequence which converges in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
modulo symmetries.

Proof:
Since the equations in (
1.6
) are the time–translation symmetric, we can
set
t_{n}=0
for all
n\geq 1
. Hence,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}S_{\geq 0}(u_{n},v_{n})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}S_{\leq 0}(u_{n},v_{n})=+\infty.

(5.53)

Using the linear profile decomposition in Lemma 5.13 to the sequence
(u_{n}(0),v_{n}(0))
, up to a subsequence,
we can get

\displaystyle u_{n}(0)=\sum_{j=1}^{J}g_{n}^{j}e^{it_{n}^{j}\Delta}\phi_{1}^{j}+\varphi_{n}^{J},\quad v_{n}(0)=\sum_{j=1}^{J}g_{n}^{j}e^{it_{n}^{j}\Delta}\phi_{2}^{j}+\psi_{n}^{J}.

Similar to the argument in the proof of Proposition 5.3 in
[
60
]
, we may assume that for each
j\geq 1
, either
t_{n}^{j}\equiv 0
or
t_{n}^{j}\rightarrow\pm\infty
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
. Similarly, we can define the nonlinear
profiles
(u^{j},v^{j}):I^{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
and
(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j}):I_{n}^{j}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
.

As the decoupling of the kinetic energy is asymptotic, we can find
J_{0}\geq 1
such
that

\|\nabla\phi^{j}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi^{j}_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\eta_{0}

for all
j\geq J_{0}
and
\eta_{0}=\eta_{0}(d)
such that the local wellposedness of the solution to (
1.6
) holds.
By Corollary 5.9, the solutions
(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})
are global and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}[\|\nabla u_{n}^{j}(t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla v_{n}^{j}(t)\|_{2}^{2}]+S_{\mathbb{R}}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})\leq\|\nabla\phi_{1}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi_{2}\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.54)

Since the kinetic
energy is not a conserved quantity,
even if
(u_{n}^{j}(0),v_{n}^{j}(0))=g_{n}^{j}(u_{n}^{j}(t_{n}^{j}),v_{n}^{j}(t_{n}^{j}))
posses kinetic energy less
than the critical value
E_{c}
, it cannot guarantee the same will hold throughout
the lifespan of
(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})
, especially, it cannot
make sure neither global existence nor
global spacetime bounds. Consequently, we have to seek for a profile
responsible for the asymptotic blowup (
5.53
) as follows.

Claim 5.15(At least one bad profile).

There exists
1\leq j_{0}<J_{0}
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup S_{[0,\sup I_{n}^{j_{0}})}(u_{n}^{j_{0}},v_{n}^{j_{0}})=+\infty.

Proof:
Assume contradictorily that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup S_{[0,\sup I_{n}^{j})}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})<+\infty,

(5.55)

which implies that
\sup I_{n}^{j}=+\infty
for all
1\leq j<j_{0}
and all
n
large enough. Using (
5.54
), (
5.55
) and (
5.52
),
we have for all
n
large enough

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 1}S_{[0,+\infty)}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})\lesssim 1+\sum_{j\geq J_{0}}[\|\nabla\phi_{1}^{j}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi_{2}^{j}\|_{2}^{2}]\lesssim 1+E_{c}.

(5.56)

To obtain the contradiction to (
5.53
), we will use the stability result and (
5.56
) to get
a bound on the scattering size of
(u_{n},v_{n})
. Therefore, we define the approximate solution
(U_{n}^{J}(t),V_{n}^{J}(t))
as

U^{J}_{n}(t):=\sum^{J}_{j=1}u_{n}^{j}(t)+e^{it\Delta}\varphi_{n}^{J},\quad V^{J}_{n}(t):=\sum^{J}_{j=1}v_{n}^{j}(t)+e^{it\Delta}\psi_{n}^{J}.

Recalling (
5.56
) and the asymptotic vanishing of the scattering size of
(e^{it\Delta}\varphi_{n}^{J},e^{it\Delta}\psi_{n}^{J})
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\lim_{J\rightarrow J^{*}}\lim_{n\rightarrow}\sup S_{[0,+\infty)}(U_{n}^{J},V_{n}^{J})

\displaystyle\lesssim\lim_{J\rightarrow J^{*}}\lim_{n\rightarrow}\sup\left(S_{[0,+\infty)}(\sum_{j=1}^{J}u_{n}^{j},\sum_{j=1}^{J}v_{n}^{j})+S_{[0,+\infty)}(e^{it\Delta}\varphi_{n}^{J},e^{it\Delta}\psi_{n}^{J})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lim_{J\rightarrow J^{*}}\lim_{n\rightarrow}\sup\sum_{j=1}^{J}S_{[0,+\infty)}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})\lesssim 1+E_{c}.

(5.57)

Moreover, we will see that
(U_{n}^{J},V_{n}^{J})
is a good approximation to
(u_{n},v_{n})
below. In fact, by the way
(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})
is constructed,

u_{n}(0)-\sum_{j=1}^{J}u_{n}^{j}(0)-\varphi_{n}^{J}\rightarrow 0,\quad v_{n}(0)-\sum_{j=1}^{J}v_{n}^{j}(0)-\psi_{n}^{J}\rightarrow 0

in
\dot{H}^{1}_{x}\times\dot{H}^{1}_{x}
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
. And by the linear decomposition in Lemma 5.13 and the definition of
(U_{n}^{J},V_{n}^{J})
, we get

Claim 5.16(Asymptotic agreement with initial data).

For any
J\geq 1
, there holds

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}[\|U^{J}_{n}(0)-u_{n}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|V^{J}_{n}(0)-v_{n}(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}]=0.

Similar to the proof of Lemma 3.2 in
[
60
]
, we also show that

Claim 5.17(Asymptotic solution to the equation).

For the approximate solution
(U_{n}^{J}(t),V_{n}^{J}(t))
,

\displaystyle\lim_{J\rightarrow J^{*}}\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\|\nabla[(i\partial_{t}+\Delta)U_{n}^{J}-f_{1}(U^{J}_{n},V^{J}_{n})]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0

\displaystyle\lim_{J\rightarrow J^{*}}\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\|\nabla[(i\partial_{t}+\Delta)V_{n}^{J}-f_{2}(U^{J}_{n},V^{J}_{n})]\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}]=0.

Proof:
By the definition of
(U_{n}^{J}(t),V_{n}^{J}(t))
, we have

\displaystyle(i\partial_{t}+\Delta)U_{n}^{J}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}f_{1}(u^{j}_{n},v^{j}_{n}),\quad(i\partial_{t}+\Delta)V_{n}^{J}=\sum_{j=1}^{J}f_{2}(u^{j}_{n},v^{j}_{n}),

by triangle inequality, it sufficient to prove that

\displaystyle\lim_{J\rightarrow J^{*}}\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\|f_{1}(U_{n}^{J}-e^{it\Delta}\varphi_{n}^{J},V_{n}^{J}-e^{it\Delta}\psi_{n}^{J})-f_{1}(U^{J}_{n},V^{J}_{n})\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{J\rightarrow J^{*}}\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\|f_{2}(U_{n}^{J}-e^{it\Delta}\varphi_{n}^{J},V_{n}^{J}-e^{it\Delta}\psi_{n}^{J})-f_{2}(U^{J}_{n},V^{J}_{n})\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\|f_{1}(\sum_{j=1}^{J}u_{n}^{j},\sum_{j=1}^{J}v_{n}^{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{J}f_{1}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0\quad{\rm for\ all}\quad J\geq 1,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\|f_{2}(\sum_{j=1}^{J}u_{n}^{j},\sum_{j=1}^{J}v_{n}^{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{J}f_{2}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})\|_{L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d+4}}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0\quad{\rm for\ all}\quad J\geq 1.

Since

\displaystyle\quad|f_{1}(u_{1},v_{1})-f_{1}(u_{2},v_{2})|+|f_{2}(u_{1},v_{1})-f_{2}(u_{2},v_{2})|

(5.58)

\displaystyle\lesssim[|u_{1}|^{\alpha+\beta+2}+|v_{1}|^{\alpha+\beta+2}+|u_{2}|^{\alpha+\beta+2}+|v_{2}|^{\alpha+\beta+2}][|u_{1}-u_{2}|+|v_{1}-v_{2}|],

(5.59)

by the asymptotically vanishing scattering size of
(e^{it\Delta}\varphi_{n}^{J},e^{it\Delta}\psi_{n}^{J})
and (
5.57
), we can obtain the first limit
and the second one are zero.

Since

\displaystyle\quad|f_{1}(\sum_{j=1}^{J}u_{n}^{j},\sum_{j=1}^{J}v_{n}^{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{J}f_{1}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})|+|f_{2}(\sum_{j=1}^{J}u_{n}^{j},\sum_{j=1}^{J}v_{n}^{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{J}f_{2}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum^{J}_{j=1,j^{\prime}=1,j\neq j^{\prime}}[|u_{n}^{j}|+|v_{n}^{j}|][|u_{n}^{j^{\prime}}|^{\alpha+\beta+2}+|v_{n}^{j^{\prime}}|^{\alpha+\beta+2}],

the third limit and the fourth one are zero by the asymptotic orthogonality of the
(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})
. The details are similar to the proof of (3.10) in Lemma 3.2 of
[
60
]
.
\Box

By the two claims above and (
5.57
), we have for
n
large enough,

S_{[0,+\infty)}(u_{n},v_{n})\lesssim 1+E_{c},

which contradicts to (
5.53
). Claim 5.15 is proved.
\Box

Let’s come back to the proof of Lemma 5.14. Rearranging the indices, we can assume that there exists

1\leq J_{1}\leq J_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup S_{[0,\sup I_{n}^{j})}(u^{j}_{n},v^{j}_{n})=+\infty\quad{\rm for}\quad 1\leq j\leq J_{1},

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup S_{[0,\sup I_{n}^{j})}(u^{j}_{n},v^{j}_{n})=+\infty\quad{\rm for}\quad j>J_{1}.

Up to a subsequence in
n
if necessary, there holds
S_{[0,\sup I_{n}^{1})}(u^{1}_{n},v^{1}_{n})=+\infty
.

Since there are maybe two or more profiles
that take turns at driving the scattering norm of
(u_{n},v_{n})
to infinity, we have to prove that only one profile is
responsible for the asymptotic blowup (
5.53
). To do this, we must
prove that kinetic energy decoupling for the nonlinear profiles for large periods of time is sufficiently large such that that the kinetic energy of
(u_{n}^{1},v^{1}_{n})
has achieved the critical one.

For each
m,n>1
, we can define a integer
j(m,n)\in\{1,...,J_{1}\}
and a time interval
K_{n}^{m}
of the form
[0,\tau]
by

\displaystyle\sup_{1\leq j\leq J}S_{K^{m}_{n}}(u_{n}^{j},v_{n}^{j})=S_{K^{m}_{n}}(u_{n}^{j(m,n)},v_{n}^{j(m,n)})=m.

(5.60)

Note that there is a
1\leq j_{1}\leq J_{1}
such that for infinitely many
m

one has
j(m,n)=j_{1}
for infinitely many
n
by the pigeonhole principle. Without loss of generality, we reorder these indices and let
j_{1}=1
. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\sup\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup\sup_{t\in K^{m}_{n}}[\|\nabla u^{1}_{n}(t)\|^{2}_{2}+\|\nabla u^{1}_{n}(t)\|^{2}_{2}]\geq E_{c},

(5.61)

where
E_{c}
is the critical kinetic energy.

Meanwhile, (
5.60
) implies that all
(u^{j}_{n},v^{j}_{n})
have finite scattering size on
K^{m}_{n}

for each
m\geq 1
. Similar to the discussions in Claim 5.15, we can find that for

n
and
J
large enough,
(U_{n}^{J},V_{n}^{J})
is a good approximation to
(u_{n},v_{n})
on each
K^{m}_{n}
for each
m\geq 1
in the following sense

\displaystyle\lim_{J\rightarrow J^{*}}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup[\|U^{J}_{n}-u_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(K^{m}_{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|V^{J}_{n}-v_{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(K^{m}_{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(5.62)

Similar to the proof of Lemma 5.10 in
[
60
]
, we can prove the following claim but we omit the details here.

Claim 5.18(Kinetic energy decoupling for
(U_{n}^{J},V_{n}^{J})
).

For all
J\geq 1
and
m\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\quad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup\sup_{t\in K^{m}_{n}}|\|\nabla U^{J}_{n}(t)\|^{2}_{2}-\sum^{J}_{j=1}\|\nabla u^{j}_{n}(t)\|^{2}_{2}-\|\nabla\varphi^{J}_{n}\|^{2}_{2}|

\displaystyle+\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup\sup_{t\in K^{m}_{n}}|\|\nabla V^{J}_{n}(t)\|^{2}_{2}-\sum^{J}_{j=1}\|\nabla v^{j}_{n}(t)\|^{2}_{2}-\|\nabla\psi^{J}_{n}\|^{2}_{2}|=0.

By this claim and using (
5.52
), (
5.62
), we find for each
m\geq 1

\displaystyle E_{c}

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup\sup_{t\in K^{m}_{n}}[\|\nabla u_{n}(t)\|^{2}_{2}+\|\nabla v_{n}(t)\|^{2}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\lim_{J\rightarrow\infty}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup\{\|\nabla\varphi_{n}^{J}\|^{2}_{2}+\|\nabla\psi_{n}^{J}\|^{2}_{2}+\sup_{t\in K^{m}_{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{J}[\|\nabla u_{n}^{j}(t)\|^{2}_{2}+\|\nabla v_{n}^{j}(t)\|^{2}_{2}].\}

Invoking (
5.61
), we can find some
g_{n}\in G
,
\tau_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
, some functions
\phi_{1}
,
\phi_{2}
,
\varphi_{n}
and
\psi_{n}
in

\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
(\varphi_{n},\psi_{n})\rightarrow(0,0)
strongly in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

such that

\displaystyle u_{n}(0)=g_{n}e^{i\tau_{n}\Delta}\phi_{1}+\varphi_{n},\quad v_{n}(0)=g_{n}e^{i\tau_{n}\Delta}\phi_{2}+\psi_{n}.

(5.63)

Moreover, similar to the proof of Proposition 5.3 in
[
60
]
, we can prove
\tau_{n}\equiv 0
. Lemma 5.14 is proved.
\Box

Lemma 5.19(Reduction to almost periodic solution)

Suppose that Theorem 6 failed when
d=3
or Theorem 7 failed when
d=4
. Then there exists a maximal-lifespan solution
(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
such that

\sup_{t\in I}[\|\nabla u(t)\|^{2}_{2}+\|\nabla v(t)\|^{2}_{2}]=E_{c},

(u,v)
is almost periodic modulo symmetric and blows up both forward and backward in time.

Proof:
By the definition of the critical energy
E_{c}
(and the
continuity of
L
), there exist a sequence
(u_{n},v_{n}):I_{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
of maximal-lifespan
solutions with
E(u_{n},v_{n})\leq E_{c}
and
\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}S_{I_{n}}(u_{n},v_{n})=+\infty
. We can chose
t_{n}\in I_{n}
to be
the median time of the
L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}
norm of
(u_{n},v_{n})

such that (
5.53
) holds. Without loss of generality, we take

t_{n}=0
because of the time-translation invariance.

Noticing Lemma 5.14 and up to a subsequence if necessary, there exist
group elements
g_{n}\in G
such that
g_{n}(u_{n}(0),v_{n}(0))
converges strongly in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to some

(u_{0},v_{0})\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
By applying the group action
T_{g_{n}}
to the solutions
(u_{n},v_{n})
, we
can take
g_{n}
to all be the identity. Consequently,
(u_{n}(0),v_{n}(0))
converge strongly in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to some

(u_{0},v_{0})
, which particularly implies that
E(u_{0},v_{0})<E_{c}
.

Let
(u,v):I\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
be the maximal-lifespan solution to (
1.6
)) with initial data

u(0)=u_{0}
and
v(0)=v_{0}
. We will show that
(u,v)
blows up both forward
and backward in time. In fact, if
(u,v)
does not blow up forward in time, then

[0,+\infty)\subseteq I
and
S_{\geq 0}(u,v)<\infty
. By energy-critical stability result, for
n

large enough, we have
[0,+\infty)\subseteq I
and

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup S_{\geq 0}(u_{n},v_{n})<+\infty,

which is a contradiction to (
5.53
). By the definition of
E_{c}
, it can derive that
E(u_{0},v_{0})\geq E_{c}
and
E(u_{0},v_{0})
have to equal
E_{c}
.

Last, we will show that the solution u is almost periodic modulo
G
. Consider
an arbitrary sequence
(u(t^{\prime}_{n}),v(t^{\prime}_{n}))
in the orbit
\{(u(t),v(t)):t\in I\}
. Although
(u,v)
blows up both
forward and backward in time, it is locally in
L^{\frac{2(d+2)}{d-2}}_{t,x}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle S_{\geq t^{\prime}_{n}}(u,v)=S_{\leq t^{\prime}_{n}}(u,v)=\infty.

By the Palais-Smale condition modulo symmetries, there exist a a convergent subsequence of
G(u(t^{\prime}_{n}),v(t^{\prime}_{n}))
in

G\setminus[\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Hence, the orbit
\{G(u(t),v(t)):t\in I\}
is precompact in
G\setminus[\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
\Box

Proof of Proposition 5.2:
Proposition 5.2 is a direct result of the conclusions above.
\Box

Now we come back to consider (
1.6
) when
d=3
below.

5.3
Strichartz estimates

In this subsection, we give some Strichartz estimates, some of their proofs can be found in
[
21
,
55
]
.

Lemma 5.20(Strichartz inequality).

Let
I
be a compact time interval and
w:I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a solution to the forced Schrödinger equation

iw_{t}+\Delta w=f.

Then

\displaystyle\{\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathcal{Z}}}\|\nabla w_{N}\|_{L^{q}_{t}L^{r}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\|w(t_{0})\|_{\dot{H}^{1}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\gamma^{\prime}}_{t}L^{\rho^{\prime}}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(5.64)

for any
t_{0}\in I
and any admissible pairs
(q,r)
and
(\gamma,\rho)
, where
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\rho^{\prime}
are the dual exponents to
\gamma
and
\rho
respectively.

Lemma 5.21(An endpoint estimate).

For any
w:I\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, the following inequality hold

\|w\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\|\nabla w\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\{\sum_{N\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}}\|\nabla w_{N}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{6}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}.

And

\|w_{\leq N}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lesssim\|\nabla w_{\leq N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\{\sum_{M\leq N}\|\nabla w_{M}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{6}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\right\}^{\frac{1}{4}}.

for any frequency
N>0
.

Proposition 5.22.

If
w
satisfies
iv_{t}+\Delta v=f+g
on a compact interval
[0,T]
, then for each
6<q\leq+\infty
,

\|M(t)^{\frac{3}{q}-1}\|P_{M(t)}w(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\||\nabla|^{-\frac{1}{2}}w\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\||\nabla|^{-\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\frac{6}{5}}}+\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}

uniformly for all functions
M:[0,T]\rightarrow 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
. Here the spacetime estimates are over
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Proposition 5.23.

If
w
satisfies
iw_{t}+\Delta w=f+g
on a compact interval
[0,T]
and

\displaystyle[\mathcal{S}_{R}w](t,x):=\left((\pi R^{2})^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v(t,x+y)|^{2}e^{-\frac{|y|^{2}}{R^{2}}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.65)

then for each
0<R<+\infty
and
6<q\leq+\infty
,

\displaystyle R^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{q}}\|\mathcal{S}_{R}w\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|w\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{1}_{x}}+\|g\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\frac{6}{5}}_{x}},

(5.66)

where the spacetime norms are over
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Lemma 5.24.

\displaystyle\sup_{M>0}M^{\frac{3}{q}-1}\|f_{M}\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\lesssim\sup_{M>0}M^{\frac{3}{q}-1}\|\mathcal{S}_{M^{-1}}(f_{M})\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\quad{\rm for\ fixed}\quad 6<q\leq+\infty.

(5.67)

Lemma 5.25.

The intergral kernel

K_{R}(\tau,z;s,y;x):=(\pi R^{2})^{-\frac{3}{2}}<\delta_{z},e^{i\tau\Delta}e^{\frac{-|\cdot-x|^{2}}{R^{2}}}e^{is\Delta}\delta_{y}>

satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{R>0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}R^{2-\frac{6}{q}}\|K_{R}(\tau,z;s,y;x)\|_{L^{\infty}_{z,y}L^{\frac{q}{2}}_{x}}f(t+\tau)f(t-s)dsd\tau\lesssim|[\mathcal{M}f](t)|^{2},

(5.68)

for fixed
6<q\leq+\infty
, where
\mathcal{M}
denotes the Hardy-Littlewood maximal operator and
f:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,+\infty)
.

Proposition 5.26(Long-time Strichartz estimate).

Assume that
(u,v):(-T_{\min},T_{\max})\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}

is a maximal-lifespan almost periodic solution to (
1.6
) with
(\alpha,\beta)=(0,2)
and
I\subset(-T_{\min},T_{\max})
a time interval which is titled by finitely many characteristic interval
J_{k}
. For any fixed
6<q<+\infty
and any frequency
N>0
, define

\displaystyle K:=\int_{I}\widetilde{N}(t)^{-1}dt,

(5.69)

\displaystyle A(N):=\{\sum_{M\leq N}\|\nabla u_{M}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{6}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|\nabla v_{M}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{6}_{x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(5.70)

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{q}(N):=N^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\sup_{M\geq N}M^{\frac{3}{q}-1}[\|u_{M}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\|v_{M}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}]\|_{L^{2}_{t}(I)}.
